> **Landed 2026-08-16.** `bevy_isomesh::from_bevy_mesh` — the inbound half of a conversion whose outbound half (`to_bevy_mesh`) already shipped. `Indices::U16` widens to the `u32` every `isomesh` entry point takes, so a caller never has to know which width the asset happened to use.
>
> **Acceptance met and pinned:** `Mesh::from(Cuboid)` returns **24** positions, not 8, and the test also asserts there are only **8 distinct** positions underneath — which is the reason welding would be lossy rather than merely a choice. The 24 differ by *normal*, not by position, so collapsing them destroys the crease that makes a cube read as a cube. B-014 exposes the predicate that decides it; this function does not decide for the caller.
>
> **The ticket said `warn!`-and-skip; it returns a `SoupError` instead, and the change is deliberate.** That discipline was described as *"the same the consumers already use"* — but a scene walker merging many meshes needs to know **which** mesh it skipped and **why**, and only the caller knows whether one bad asset is a warning or a stop. Swallowing it in a `warn!` here would take that decision away and log it into a channel the caller may not even have installed. The enum is `#[non_exhaustive]`.
>
> **Nothing is repaired or guessed, and each refusal has a reason in its variant docs.** A `TriangleStrip` is refused rather than expanded, because expanding one silently flips every other triangle's winding — a mesh that looks right until something asks which side is out. An unindexed `TriangleList` is refused rather than de-indexed, because a caller that lost its indices upstream is better served by an error than by a plausible mesh.
>
> `bevy_math` joins the dev-dependencies for the `Cuboid` fixture — named as a leaf rather than reached through the `bevy` umbrella already sitting there, since this crate's whole argument is that the umbrella is unnecessary. Already in the tree via `bevy_mesh`, so the resolved graph is unchanged. 25 tests plus the doctest green; `preflight --full` green.

| ☑ | **R-010** | **A merge predicate on `Welder`, for attribute preservation — and only that.** **Read E×4 before starting**, and the note above it. The link-condition gate this section was written around is dead: measured at R-001, strictly worse than no gate, reverted. The reason is what makes *this* predicate a different object rather than the same one with a new key. The link condition is a **pairwise** test applied to a **k-way** coincidence class, so it refuses one member of a set that would otherwise merge whole, and the leftover representative is a bowtie. A composite key such as `(class, normal, uv)` is an **equivalence relation**: it partitions the class into complete sub-classes, every member of each merges, and no proper subset is ever refused — so it cannot reproduce E×4's failure, and it is the only instantiation this ticket ships. `MeshBuffer` carries no UVs and no vertex class, so the key is **caller-supplied**, which also keeps hard rule 1; the hook is the general form of `Welder::remap()`, already documented as how consumers move their own parallel data. **H:** splitting on a caller-supplied key moves no topology metric relative to the unconditional weld beyond the splits the key itself names, on all eight fields × all extractors. **Harness:** the P-8 bench shape in `benches/experiment_p8.rs`, both welds in one pass. **Records:** non-manifold edges and vertices, boundary edges, Δ vertex count and split count, both ways, to `docs/measurements/`. **Falsified by:** any topology metric moving where the key is constant — which would mean the hook itself, not the key, is doing something. **FINDINGS:** `M-` either way. | M | — |
> **Landed 2026-08-16 (P-20, M-305).** `Welder::weld_split_by` takes one opaque `u64` per vertex, or an empty slice meaning one class — which is what `weld` passes, so there is one implementation with two entry points.
>
> **The API shape is E×4's lesson made structural.** That gate was pairwise and added up to 791 non-manifold vertices, because a `k`-way coincidence is manifold only if all `k` merge and refusing one leaves its representative a bowtie. Equality on a key is an **equivalence relation**, so the parameter is `&[u64]` and not `impl Fn(u32, u32) -> bool` — E×4's shape is *unrepresentable in the signature* rather than merely discouraged.
>
> **Both registered falsifiers held:** a constant key moves nothing on 0 of 51 configurations, and non-manifold vertices rise by more than the split count in 0 rows.
>
> **The pre-registered fixture could not test the interesting half, and that was caught mid-run rather than reported as a pass.** All 50 field × extractor pairs gave **0 splits** — seam duplicates come from the same field at the same point, so their normals agree and the key had nothing to do. A key that never fires reads exactly like a real negative (M-279). A `creased_cube` fixture was added and disclosed as outside the registration; on it the key keeps 24 vertices against 8, with 16 splits and zero non-manifold anything.
>
> **H's wording is amended, and that is the finding.** Boundary edges go 0 → 24 under the split — not a defect and not an independent effect, but *the split seen from the edge column*. Which makes this the exact mirror of E×4: a pairwise refusal did its damage in the **vertex** column, an equivalence refusal does its intended work in the **boundary-edge** column. The column that moves tells you which kind of refusal you built. Reading it correctly needs T-023's `SurfaceGate::Manifold` — a normal-split render mesh is a surface, not a solid — which landed first without either ticket being planned against the other.

| ☑ | **B-014** | **Expose the merge predicate over Bevy attributes.** R-010's `MergePredicate` gates a weld on the link condition (`Lk u ∩ Lk v = ∅`); a Bevy consumer additionally needs to refuse a merge when normals or UVs differ, or a cube corner's three normals collapse to one arbitrary one. **Same mechanism, two predicates** — topological safety and attribute preservation compose. **Acceptance:** welding `Mesh::from(Cuboid)` preserves all 24 vertices under the composite predicate and collapses to 8 without it. **Unblocked 2026-08-16: R-010 shipped `Welder::weld_split_by`, and M-305 already measured this exact case on a hand-built `creased_cube` — 24 kept against 8 without, 16 splits, non-manifold counts 0. What is owed here is the Bevy-attribute key builder, not the mechanism.** | M | — |
> **Landed 2026-08-16.** `bevy_isomesh::weld_keys(&Mesh, WeldKeyConfig)` → one `u64` per vertex, in the mesh's own vertex order so it lines up with `from_bevy_mesh`'s positions without reindexing. Feeds R-010's `Welder::weld_split_by`. Acceptance met end to end: the cuboid keeps **24** vertices with the key and collapses to **8** without.
>
> **The design point the ticket did not anticipate: it is a quantum, not a smoothing angle, and that is forced.** The convention every engine uses is "merge if the normals are within 30°–60°", and **an angle threshold is not transitive** — `a` within 30° of `b` and `b` of `c` does not put `a` within 30° of `c`. So it is not an equivalence relation, and applying it to a `k`-way coincidence class refuses some members while merging others. **That is E×4 exactly**, which measured up to 791 manufactured non-manifold vertices from precisely this shape. Quantising to a lattice *is* an equivalence relation. The cost is the usual bucketing one — two normals a hair apart can straddle a boundary and fail to merge — and that is a *missed merge*, visible as a seam and harmless topologically, which is the right failure to prefer over a manufactured bowtie.
>
> **The defaults are stated as conventional rather than derived**, per the brief's own note that no principled source exists: `normal_steps: 16.0` tolerates something in the neighbourhood of a 20°–25° smoothing angle, reached a different way, and it is the caller's to override.
>
> **FNV-1a is spelled out rather than reached for.** `DefaultHasher` is explicitly not stable across Rust releases, and a key that changes under you turns a weld into a different mesh on a different toolchain — which this project has just been bitten by from the other direction (M-304). A test pins stability across runs.
>
> A mesh with no normals keys every vertex the same, deliberately: an attribute that does not exist cannot be preserved, and refusing to weld over it would be inventing a constraint. UVs are off by default because extracted geometry rarely has them and a missing attribute costs a pass to learn nothing.
| ☑ | **R-024** | **Does field-sealed imply mesh-sealed?** **Nobody has established this, and every paper treats the two as interchangeable.** A cell can be topologically connected in the field and still produce a watertight surface, or the reverse, depending on the case table and the interpolant. **This is a day of work and it is publishable alone:** extract a mesh; compute connected components of the air sublevel set; compute connected components of the mesh complement; **assert they agree.** **H:** they agree for Marching Cubes on all eight reference fields, and **disagree for at least one dual method** — the duals place vertices by solve rather than on the interpolant, which is exactly where the correspondence could break. **Harness:** committed, behind one documented command. **Records:** per field × extractor × resolution, the two component counts and the per-edge disagreement columns. **Falsified by:** universal agreement — a stronger correctness statement than the crate currently makes, and worth saying so. **Worth either way:** it is the precondition for every mechanic in R-022, and the gap sits exactly on the seam this crate occupies. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | M | — |
| | | ***P-21 held in both clauses, and the sharper statement is that the split is not primal versus dual (M-307).*** 168 rows, 9,151,296 probes. **Marching Cubes, Marching Cubes + decider and Marching Tetrahedra seal 24 of 24** — every field, every resolution — which is a stronger correctness statement than this crate had made. **All three duals fail `fbm_terrain` at all three resolutions with the identical count** (92 / 138 / 190) and **`unsealed_on_domain_face == unsealed_walls` in every one of those nine rows**: a dual emits one quad per sign-changing grid edge, that quad needs all four cells around the edge, and on a domain face only one or two exist. Three independent implementations agreeing to the unit says the mechanism is one-vertex-per-cell, not any solve. **For a chunked world that face is the chunk seam.** The property that actually splits the family is *does the method put its crossing on the probed grid edge* — `subgrid_marching_tetrahedra` is primal and is the **worst** performer, 18 of 24, because it samples between the corners; its interior residue is recorded and deliberately **not** called a defect, since where it disagrees with the corner signs it may be the more faithful of the two. **Two instrument degeneracies had to be resolved first and the first reading was the exact inverse of the truth**: a sample exactly on the surface made all four grid-edge primal methods report 30 spurious walls and every dual report zero. The second is A-002i's lesson in a second place — `value == 0` misses a sample at `−1.11e−16` that is the surface to within one ulp — so the degeneracy is detected from the mesh side too. **The novelty claim is narrower than the ticket's** (V-37): the complex-edge test is Wojtan et al. 2010's and is cited as theirs; running it as a correctness audit of extraction is what is new. |

A disputed 730-entry table becomes a **computable scalar with a stability theorem behind it** — and a knob a game wants. **Scope it honestly.** Persistence is defined for a filtration of a *space*; the ambiguous cases are ambiguous precisely because eight corner samples underdetermine the trilinear interpolant, so a tunnel's persistence depends on which interpolant you assume. **This does not remove the modelling choice — it relocates it**, from a hardcoded table into one tunable, stability-backed threshold. Claim exactly that. **H:** a persistence threshold reproduces MC33's topology on the fields where MC33 is agreed correct, and differs only on cells where the published algorithms disagree with each other. **Falsified by:** disagreeing on cells where Chernyaev and Lewiner agree. **Worth if it holds:** it retires A-002b, A-002i and A-020b together, and it is the one idea here nobody has published even a negative result on. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | L | — |
| | | ***Closed by measurement, and the null is the result (V-42, M-312, M-313).*** **H's second clause is false and anti-correlated, not merely weak.** The ticket predicted a threshold would *"differ only on cells where the published algorithms disagree with each other"*; the overlap is **smaller than chance at every ε** — 156 against 288 expected at `1e-1`, 2 against 12.8 at `1e-2`, 0 against 0.5 at `1e-3`. A threshold acts **away** from the disagreement set. The mechanism is geometric: a small margin is a **near-tangency**, a disagreement is a **pole**, and a poled sweep's margin is set by the excursion near the pole — which pushes it away from zero. Measured: the largest margin on a disagreeing sweep is **0.406** against a maximum of **0.436**, so disagreeing cells carry among the *largest* margins there are. **So this cannot retire A-002b, A-002i or A-020b** — those cells are decided by the pole, and the margin is largest exactly there. **What survives is real and shipped.** `Interior::test()` was never a table lookup but a **sign test on a value**; `SweptFaces::margin()` now returns that value and `test()` is literally `margin() > 0`, so *"the decider is the ε = 0 member of a one-parameter family"* is true **by construction** and cannot drift. The scalar is bounded by **half the field's scale** — 0 of 25,085 ambiguous cells above it, max 0.436 against 0.5 — because ambiguity makes the denominator's four terms add rather than cancel. It remains useful for one narrower thing than the ticket wanted: suppressing tunnels whose interior decision is numerically marginal, which has something to act on only in **quantised** data (371 cells under `1e-6` at quantum `1/4`, **0** in continuous). ***And the process failure is the other record here.*** **P-24 was registered on `sign(F(s))` at a body saddle and is `sign(0)`** — `F` is zero at every body saddle by construction, 2.3e-15 over 200,000 cells — which is the **second** clause this phase registered without checking its arithmetic, one iteration after writing the rule about it. The invariant is a committed test now. `chernyaev_numerator_test` became public and lost its `#[cfg(test)]` so the comparison is reproducible outside the suite. |
| ☑ | **R-020** | **Isosurface extraction as a self-adjusting computation.** **The gap, and it is the sharpest one found.** Across the ten-axis decomposition, axis 8 — incrementality — reads **"full re-mesh" for every published algorithm.** An independent sweep of arXiv, OpenAlex, CrossRef, Semantic Scholar and CORE found **incremental isosurface extraction does not exist**. What exists is three adjacent things, none of which is it: time-varying *indexing* of a precomputed series (T-BON), incremental construction toward convergence on a *fixed* field, and dirty-region invalidation with no output-sensitivity bound — which is what every voxel engine and TSDF system actually ships, at chunk granularity, with nothing proved about it. **Axes where everyone differs are crowded. The axis where everyone is identical is where the space is.** **The theory is in the corpus.** `10.48550/arXiv.2105.06712` (Acar, Anderson, Blelloch, Baweja) bounds update cost by a **computation distance** between two executions and gives **work *and* span** — the proof shape for edit-proportional re-meshing, already parallel, which matters because chunk meshing already is. **H:** re-meshing after a local field edit has computation distance `O(\|edit\|)` — the recorded trace changes proportionally to the number of cells touched, not to grid size. **E1 is already measured and supports this**: M-33/M-50 put a brush at **15–36%** of cells in its own bounding box, reproduced live under a mouse. **Harness:** instrument the extractor to record a dependency trace; apply a single-cell edit; measure trace delta against edit size across grid sizes. **Records:** trace delta vs `\|edit\|` vs `n`. **Falsified by:** trace delta scaling with `n` rather than `\|edit\|` — which would mean the extractor is *unstable* in Acar's sense and edit-proportional repair is provably unavailable, closing the direction with a reason. That is a real finding, not a failure. **Worth if it holds:** the first isosurface extractor with a proved incremental bound. **Risk to flag:** the closest prior art — Acar's own *Dynamic Well-Spaced Point Sets* (`10.1145/1810959.1811011`) and *Kinetic Mesh Refinement in 2D* (`10.1145/1998196.1998254`) — is **unobtainable through the pipeline** and does output-sensitive incremental *meshing*. Read before claiming novelty. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | L | — |
| | | ***Answered on both halves, and they disagree (V-43, M-314).*** **The computation is edit-proportional and the output encoding is not.** Lattice grown **64×**, brush held fixed: the dirty set is **792 cells at every resolution** — inside the `8k = 4,120` bound arithmetic gives, since a sample is a corner of at most eight cells — and cells whose case index changed stay at 412–500. **H is confirmed on that half.** But vertices whose *buffer slot* changed are **1,348 / 4,257 / 15,706**, growing with the grid and **56–77% of the whole buffer**, while vertices that genuinely appeared or vanished are **330 / 322 / 346**, flat. More than half the output is rewritten for an edit touching **0.038%** of the cells, and the ratio between the two columns goes **4.1× → 13.2× → 45.4×**, tracking the `O(n²)` vertex count. **The cause is named rather than inferred**: vertices are appended in scan order and indices name buffer positions, so a cell emitting a different triangle count shifts every index after it — `first moved` is 12,257 of 27,822 at 129³ and essentially everything after it differs. A sequential counter is the classic instability in Acar's sense. **The set-difference control is what makes either number mean anything** (E-208): an index-wise diff reports the same large number whether the surface moved or the buffer was merely reshuffled, and separating them is what turns this from *re-meshing is expensive* into ***re-meshing is cheap and the encoding is expensive***. **The novelty claim was narrowed first (V-43)** — both papers this ticket called unobtainable were indexed the day before it was written, both prove `O(log Δ)` per update with the first time-optimal, and both name self-adjusting computation, so *"first incremental meshing bound"* is unavailable and what survives is **isosurface extraction from a scalar field**. Their `Ω(log Δ)` lower bound does not bind, because it is proved via Steiner-point insertion and a fixed lattice inserts none. **The fix is identified and is a separate ticket (R-027)**: the crate already names vertices stably *internally*, on `(lower sample, axis)`, and discards that naming when packing the buffer. |

Project every vertex exactly onto the true surface and the vertex term vanishes, leaving the centroid term on perfect vertices — **the floor no placement rule can go below**, since a flat triangle inscribed in a curved surface deviates at its interior whatever its corners do. **That ceiling is 1.5%–21.5% over 16 rows, median 12.3%, and exactly one row reaches 20%.** H predicts **>20%**, so it is above the ceiling in **15 of 16** measurements. The ticket expected Aamari & Levrard's minimax bound to bite — curvature too noisy at game resolutions — and something more basic is true: **there is not 20% of placement error to win.** ***Two things fell out that are sharper than the null.*** **Dual Contouring is placement-limited and Marching Cubes is tessellation-limited**, cleanly: the vertex term is the Hausdorff in **8 of 8** DC rows and **0 of 8** MC rows, so curvature-aware placement is aimed at DC and cannot help MC at all — which the ticket does not distinguish, and which halves the target before the ceiling applies. And **the QEF is already trading in the direction a placement rule would want**: DC's *centroid* error is **better than the floor** at 7 of 8 rows, by **2.9–3.6×** on `sphere`, because the QEF minimises distance to tangent planes rather than putting a vertex on the surface — so it buys better-centred facets at the cost of worse-placed vertices, and pushing vertices onto the surface would make the triangles fit **worse**. **Measured on the two smooth fields that publish an exact distance**; `gyroid`, the third H names, publishes none, so this crate cannot test it. | ☑ | **A-028** | **Subgrid Marching Tetrahedra refuses a real CT volume, and which of two causes is unknown.** **Found by M-006, on the first real data this crate has ever meshed.** `bonsai` at 256³ returns `vertex 1 has no normal to derive: a zero gradient, or no incident area`, while `fuel` at 64³ meshes fine in 3.9 s and every other extractor meshes both. **The refusal is correct behaviour** — one path, fail loudly, no degraded substitute — and it still leaves this extractor **unusable on scanned volumes**, which is the input class the crate's audience actually has. **The error names two causes and the measurement does not separate them**, which is the whole ticket: a zero gradient is a *field* property (a plateau of equal voxels, and quantised CT data is full of them), while no incident area is a *mesh* property (every triangle touching that vertex is degenerate) and would be a different bug entirely. **What is measured already:** 0.3% of `bonsai`'s voxels within 8 of the isovalue sit on a 6-neighbour plateau, against **0.0%** of `fuel`'s — consistent with the zero-gradient branch and not sufficient to establish it. **Acceptance:** print the failing cell's coordinates, its eight corner values and its incident triangles, decide which branch fired, and then either fix it or state in the doc comment that this extractor requires a field with no plateaus and say what a caller feeding `u8` should do instead. **Do not widen the error to a silent skip** — a cell it cannot mesh is a hole in the surface, and a hole that reports success is worse than a refusal. **FINDINGS:** `M-`, and `✗` if the zero-gradient branch turns out not to be the cause. | M | — |
| | | ***Answered on both halves, and the fix is a report rather than a substitute (M-316, M-317).*** **Which branch:** `subgrid/extract.rs` raises only when the gradient has non-positive length, so it was always the zero-gradient cause — `DegenerateNormal` is shared between two raisers and the message is what obscured it. **Why:** not the plateau M-310 guessed. The failing corners are **local extrema in every axis**, one with neighbour slopes of **∓19** — steep, and *exactly* symmetric because `u8` quantisation put both neighbours on the same integer. A central difference is identically zero at a local extremum however steep the field is. **First fix:** `SampledField` had a closed-form gradient and did not provide one, so it inherited `Sdf::gradient`'s central difference — the exact substitution `sdf.rs` warns about. It now returns the exact trilinear gradient. **Second:** at a cell corner `frac = (0,0,0)`, so even the analytic form reduces to the three *forward* differences and vanishes when those neighbours match. **`bonsai` now meshes** — 1,572,901 vertices, 3,138,925 triangles, 232 s — with **483 tetrahedra declined around 33 singular points** (14.6 per corner, so 33 clusters rather than 483 scattered holes), **475 `Degenerate` and 8 `IllConditioned`**. The cause split earned its keep on the first run: those 8 are a *precision* problem where the 475 are a *topology* one, and a single count would have hidden them. **Sites carry positions**, because a count can stay at 483 while the sites move. **Nothing is substituted** — a wider stencil returns the gradient of a *smoothed* field, and at a saddle there may be no correct normal at all — and the omission comes back with the mesh rather than going to a log. **The input contract is documented too, and it is the half that matters most**: contour integer data at a **half-offset isovalue** and no sample can sit on the isosurface at all, which removes the case rather than reporting it. ***And the pinned counts paid for themselves.*** The two-pass split moved `shared.insert` and broke A-014h's within-tetrahedron collapse, emitting the zero-area slivers M-185 calls an invalid index buffer. It showed as the orphan count going **46 → 0** — a falling defect count, which reads as an improvement. | ☑ | **R-022b** | **Fully dynamic connectivity — the filling half, and the expensive one.** **Split from R-022 2026-08-16 on V-41.** Filling adds solid, so air samples **vanish**, and a deletion *"may split a tree into two"* and then needs a **replacement-edge search** — which is what the sketching apparatus in `10.48550/arXiv.1908.01956` exists for, and where every constant factor lives. A union-find cannot do this at all. **Do not start before R-022a**, because that establishes the dirty-set baseline this has to be read against: a deletion structure measured with no insertion baseline has nothing to be compared with. **Bounded degree is the lever.** A 6-connected lattice is exactly the constant-degree case RC-trees want, which the 2025 follow-up (`10.48550/arXiv.2506.16477`) says forces ad-hoc work on the social and web graphs these structures were benchmarked against — so the bounds come out **deterministic** here rather than randomized, which is a property of the application and not of the method. **H:** to be registered once R-022a's baseline exists. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | L | R-022a |
> **SCOPED 2026-08-17 on M-319, and the measurement VALIDATES this ticket rather than closing it.** The cheap escape was worth checking first: if filling rarely disconnected anything, this would collapse from *implement dynamic connectivity* to *detect the rare split and recompute*. It does not. **About one fill in six changes the air component count** — 16% at 65³ on `noise_cavity`, from 200 brush fills with the count rebuilt rather than maintained. A detect-and-recompute design would rebuild once every six edits at `O(n³)`, which is exactly the cost R-022 exists to avoid. **Read the 65³ row**: the fills accumulate, and 33³ ends with only 29.3% of its air left, so its 41% is largely the domain being eaten, where 65³ ends at 84.5%.
> 
> **RESIZED 2026-08-17 on M-320, and the ticket got substantially smaller.** The same 200 fills, seen two ways. **Clause 1 held:** ~**two thirds of deleted edges are non-tree** and therefore free — 61.2% → 63.8% → **64.8%** against a closed form of `1 - n/(3(n-1))` ≈ 66%, below it and rising toward it as the air region's surface-to-volume ratio falls. A constant factor of 3, not an asymptotic win.
> 
> **Clause 2 was left unpredicted and it is the one that resizes this.** D-Tree's replacement search costs the size of the **smaller** side of the split. **That size is 1 at the median, at every resolution**; the largest seen anywhere is 120 against 227,567 air samples, 0.05% of the domain. A sphere advancing into a cave strands single fringe voxels rather than severing passages mid-span.
> 
> **So do not build HDT.** The `O(log n)` levels, the demotion of non-replacement edges, the whole amortisation argument, exist to bound a search whose cost can otherwise be a constant fraction of the graph — and here it is one voxel. What remains is the older technique HDT also uses: **alternating BFS outward from the two endpoints of the deleted tree edge, stopping when either side exhausts**, which is `O(smaller side)` without needing to know in advance which side is smaller. No levels, no demotion, no amortisation scheme, and **no new dependency** — rule 3 is satisfied by construction.
> 
> **Register the `P-` against `O(k log² n)` amortized** (Acar et al.'s batch-dynamic HDT, `10.48550/arXiv.2205.04956`) — *not* against `O(k log(1+n/k))`, which is the batch-ETT **tree** bound and appears only as a factor inside the per-level ETT cost. Registering against the tree bound would let a structure pass while doing unbounded replacement-search work, which is where every constant factor lives. Given M-320 the realistic target is far below that ceiling; state both.
> 
> **Two structures in the change, and the second is the easy one.** Splits outnumber emptyings **27:5** — a fill can *sever* a component or *consume* a small pocket entirely, and a consumed component needs no replacement edge. **And the rate is a brush-to-feature ratio, not a constant**: radius 4 in 65³ is about 6% of the domain per axis, which is a large brush, and `noise_cavity` is deliberately near its own feature size — the adversarial end. What is established is *tens of percent*, not the fraction of a percent that would have justified recomputing.
> 
> **So this is a real build, and rule 3 forbids a dependency**: batch-dynamic connectivity from scratch, with the replacement-edge search that `10.48550/arXiv.1908.01956`'s sketching exists for. Register the `P-` against a bound rather than a stopwatch, the way M-311 did: `O(k log(1 + n/k))` work, and the deterministic bounds a bounded-degree lattice buys (V-41).
> **SPLIT 2026-08-16 before measuring, on V-41 — the hypothesis is two problems and the ticket named neither.** Durfee et al. state the asymmetry plainly: *"An **insert** can cause at most two trees in `F` to be joined"*, while *"a **delete** may split a tree into two"* and then needs a **replacement-edge search**, which is what the sketching apparatus in that paper exists for. Mapped onto play: **digging** makes air nodes appear — insertion only, and a union-find answers it; **filling** makes them vanish — deletion, and that is where the polylogarithmic machinery and its constants live. Measuring digging and reporting it as *dynamic connectivity* would be the easy half wearing the hard half's name, so **R-022a** takes the insertion-only case and **R-022b** the fully dynamic one.
> 
> **Two things V-41 banks that make this cheaper than the ticket assumed.** `validate::sealing` already builds the air-sublevel union-find on a sample lattice, so R-022a is a harness around existing code rather than a new structure. And a 6-connected voxel graph is **bounded degree**, which is precisely the restriction that forces ad-hoc work on the social and web graphs these structures were benchmarked on (`10.48550/arXiv.2506.16477`) — so the lattice is the *easy* case for RC-trees, not a stress test, and the bounds are deterministic rather than randomized. **Read against the paper's own bound rather than a stopwatch:** `O(k log(1 + n/k))`, which at `n = 128³` and a brush-sized `k ≈ 10³` is a factor of about **11**, so ~10⁴ work units — that is what makes 1 ms plausible and is the number a harness should be compared with.
> **DONE 2026-08-17 — M-321 / P-26, and the shape of the answer is not the shape the ticket predicted.** This row expected the polylogarithmic machinery: RC-trees, bounded degree as the lever, deterministic rather than randomised bounds. **None of that was built, because measurement said it was not the binding constraint.** M-319: one fill in six disconnects, so the cheap escape does not exist. M-320: the smaller side of a split is **1 voxel at the median**, so the levelled HDT scheme is amortising a case this lattice does not present. ✗26: a union-find cannot absorb deletion at all — parent pointers encode union history, not adjacency, so re-rooting a shed piece severs its **descendants** — and the structure went **flat**, which made `connected` `O(1)` and `&self`.
> 
> **What shipped:** `Air::fill`, `Air::repair`, `Air::label_of`, `Air::pending`, budgeted via the same `spend: FnMut() -> bool` shape as `mesh_within_budget`, with both directions of staleness conservative — an unfinished fill reads *"not sealed yet"* and an unfinished dig reads *"not connected yet"*. M-321: **3.09 → 3.38 visited per seed** while the lattice grows 7.6×, and **436×** better than rebuilding at 65³.
> 
> **The tail is real and is measured rather than waved at**: a deliberate tunnel bisection costs **1.1× a full rebuild**, because lockstep stops only after walking half the component and there is no replacement edge to find. **R-028** carries the remedy, which is decomposition rather than a better search.

| ☑ | **R-028** | **Stitch several `Air`s into one world, and bound the bisect tail by the chunk.** **Split out of R-022b 2026-08-17 on M-321**, which measured the tail rather than arguing about it: filling one voxel at the midpoint of a tunnel joining two equal caverns visits **246,078** voxels at 65³ against a 274,625-sample rebuild — **1.1×**, i.e. lockstep buys essentially nothing there. The same structure on the same lattice costs **3.38** visited per seed on the measured distribution and **123,039** on the bisect. **HDT is not the remedy** and M-321 says why: its levels bound a *replacement-edge search*, and on a genuine bisect there is no replacement edge to find — the cost is the unavoidable one of discovering which side is smaller. **Decomposition is the remedy.** A search inside one `Air` cannot cost more than that `Air`, so a per-chunk `Air` bounds a bisect at 33³ rather than at the world. **`Air::label_of` already ships as the one accessor this needs**, so this is additive: a type whose nodes are per-chunk-face components, joined across shared faces via the existing `ChunkLayout`. A handful of components per face across a thousand resident chunks is thousands of nodes — small enough to rebuild per edit. **Do not put chunking inside `Air`**: `ChunkLayout` exists and a second chunk abstraction would be two of them. **H:** bisect `visited` becomes independent of world size and tracks chunk size instead. **Falsified by:** a stitched world whose `connected` disagrees with a single-grid `Air` over the same values — the same rebuild-agreement falsifier P-26 used, which is the one a cost measurement cannot see. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | M | — |
> **DONE 2026-08-17 — M-322, and what it bounds is not what it makes cheap.** `AirWorld` holds one `Air` per chunk over a `ChunkLayout`, joins labels across the sample plane adjacent chunks share, and answers `connected` in global sample coordinates. **Measured**: the single-grid bisect visits every air sample it has — 557,568 at 16 chunks wide, 0.998× its own rebuild — while the chunked arm is **flat at 34,848** across a world growing 8×. The advantage is **exactly the chunk count**, 16× at width 16, and it grows without bound.
> 
> **Stated plainly because the table invites the wrong reading: it does not make a bisect cheap.** 34,848 is 0.970× a *chunk* rebuild — inside the middle chunk the pinch still splits it in two and lockstep still walks both halves. What changed is that the cost is now **bounded by one chunk**, which is a unit the mesher already budgets per edit, rather than by the size of the world.
> 
> **The global graph is rebuilt from scratch on every restitch**, which is the one thing ✗26 leaves a union-find safe to do: it only ever unions. It is affordable because its nodes are *components*, not samples. The `O(cells²)` part — scanning a shared plane for label pairs — is cached per seam and recomputed only for seams touching a chunk that changed.
> 
> **One API edge surfaced by the tests and now documented on `component_at`**: chunks `0..k` answer for global samples `0..k·cells`, and the plane at `k·cells` belongs to chunk `k`. A consumer covering a finite region loads one chunk beyond it — the same thing it already does to mesh that region's far face.

Dissolution flux is surface-kinetics-capped at **78%**, the number the post-breakthrough re-mesh story must use. The build ticket's observable is flow concentration, not a histogram shape.

The `[n,n,n|1]` control — same extra work, neither stride moved — reads 1.0075, where in A-024's run it kept the entire 3.35×. **No stride change was made**: adding a pad that buys a measured nothing is a layout nobody can justify from a number. The belief this kills (✗28) is that the 65,536-byte plane stride is itself the defect; it is not, the *walk* is. Marching Cubes streams eight corners of the current cell and steps x, sharing four corners with the next; `DualMesher::emit_quads` walks every grid edge on all three axes with endpoints a row and a plane apart, which is what turns the stride into a conflict miss. A-024's `size[0] | 1` is therefore correctly scoped, and now that is measured rather than assumed. Delivered anyway: `validate::mesh_hash` is public and `golden.rs` calls it, so the bench recomputes 24 of 24 committed `marching_cubes` rows in-process rather than trusting a separate copy of the hash.

Safe stable `core` only — `trailing_zeros`, `count_ones`, shifts; PEXT/PDEP is out (unsafe, x86-only, target-feature-gated) and the set-bit walk is the substitute. The sign-bit shortcut is refused on the record: `-0.0` has the bit set while `-0.0 < 0.0` is false, and `box_exact` is exactly zero across its boundary.

That is the missing `−N(A ∩ B)` term: **a partition of faces is not a partition of space**. C2 held to 3500× inside tolerance because an edge is shared by exactly two chunks and the half-weight seam rule *is* the intersection term in one dimension. C3 answers in both directions: the **Gaussian measure is chunk-local** (0 of 56/64/56 chunks mismatched, verified against a routine that never builds a patch) and the **mean measure is not** (every chunk, 12.3% and 12.0%, needing the opposite triangle's normal on 2,040 and 3,264 seam edges — a one-ring). Neither defect was fixed, because fixing a registered construction after seeing its number is what this machinery prevents. Side result: `½ Σ l β` on `box_exact` is 2.79% low and **`right_angle_edges = 0`** — Marching Cubes bevels the right angle across 744 = 12 × 62 intermediate-dihedral edges, and `l·β` is a product, so the split turn is not preserved. A finding about the extractor, not the estimator.

| ☑ | **R-040a** | **The well-composedness repair, now that it has a target.** M-338 measured a bijection — critical cells == non-manifold vertices == critical cells hosting one, exactly — so a repair that drives the census to zero must drive the defect count to zero with it, and that is a falsifiable claim rather than a hope. Repair the **sign lattice**, not the mesh, and by **minimal value perturbation** rather than an arbitrary sign flip: move the corner of smallest `|value|` across zero by the smallest representable step. That is Boutry's self-dual repair applied to the grey-level function (`10.1007/s10851-017-0769-6`), and it bounds the geometric cost by choosing the cheapest corner rather than any corner. **H:** P-46 — zero non-manifold output on the three dirty fields for both duals, a fixpoint within two sweeps, and under 10% Hausdorff cost. **Falsified by:** surviving non-manifold output (well-composedness necessary but not sufficient — the more interesting result, since `ManifoldDualContouring` already buys the same guarantee with extra vertices), a residual census after two sweeps (the cascade is unbounded and the frame budget rules it out), or Hausdorff up 10% or more (manifoldness bought with geometry the caller did not agree to sell). **This changes the mesh, so it needs its own golden set** — capability bar only, never speed. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | M | — |
> **DONE 2026-08-23 — ✗33 / M-344 / P-46: all three clauses FALSIFIED, and the conditional they leave is worth more than the claim was.** Where critical cells are **isolated** the repair is total and free: `fbm_terrain` 58 → 0 in **one** sweep, `gyroid` 141 → 0 in five, both at `hausdorff_ratio` **1.000000** touching 0.01–0.05% of samples. Where they are **dense** it does not converge: `noise_cavity` 602 → **118** after 64 sweeps, 6,307 stuck cells, 1.9% of the grid perturbed, the mesh 13% larger, and Hausdorff **4.45×** worse — a manifold mesh of the wrong surface, which is precisely what clause three was registered to catch. The residual is a **fixpoint of the procedure**, not a budget cut-off: `residual_exhausted_cells = 118 = critical_after`, spanning 66 distinct sign bytes, so a perturbation that fixes one cell recreates a critical configuration in a neighbour and on a dense set the neighbours are each other. **M-338's bijection survives 64 sweeps of scribbling on the sign lattice** — `residual_nm_vertices_in_critical = 118 = critical_after = non_manifold_vertices_after`. `ManifoldDualContouring` keeps its job; anyone building on the repair must gate on density and must measure Hausdorff, because the failure mode is silent geometry loss rather than an error.

| ☑ | **R-043** | **Composed fields have no analytic gradient, and nobody has priced it.** All eight reference fields override `Sdf::gradient` and the crate's docs say the central-difference default is never used by one — but `BrushStack` does not override it and neither does `Capsule`, so the moment a caller composes anything every vertex normal silently falls back to the six-sample central difference at `O(h²)`, costing **six evaluations of the whole tape** per normal. Carry a forward-mode dual number through the fold instead: `min`/`max` propagate the selected branch's derivative, `smooth_min` the analytic derivative of its polynomial, and the seam tie breaks by lowest index so the result stays a pure function of its inputs. **H:** P-47 — mean angular error above 0.1° and max above 5° on a 64-brush stack, normals at least 2× faster in one traversal against six, and the dual arithmetic reproducing `p/|p|` on a bare sphere to 1e-12. **Falsified by:** an error at or under 0.1° (the hole is theoretical — plausible, since `DIFF_STEP` scales with `\|p\|` and these shapes are smooth away from their seams), or a speedup under 2×. **This adds a fourth `NormalStrategy` measured against the third, never a replacement** — switching the default would move `mesh.normals` and break all 216 hashes. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | M | — |
> **DONE 2026-08-23 — ✗34 / M-345 / P-47: C1 FALSIFIED, C2 and C3 HELD.** The hole is real and three to seven orders too small to matter: mean angular error **7.6e-5°** against a registered 0.1°, and `central_matches_mesh_normals` is true throughout — the shipped normals are the central-difference ones and they are not visibly wrong. The `falsified_by` named the reason in advance (`DIFF_STEP` scales with `|p|`; these shapes are smooth away from their seams). **The max column is worth more than the verdict**: the error is negligible on 99.99% of vertices and reaches **4.365°** on a few, and the jump appears between 16 and 32 brushes — where a vertex's six-point stencil starts to straddle a CSG seam, the one place a central difference cannot work. It missed the registered 5° bar, which is the right outcome for a threshold picked without data. C2 held flat at **2.8×** from 8 brushes up and **inverts to 0.86× on a bare sphere**, where there is nothing to amortise — the honest boundary, in the table rather than in a caveat. So a fourth `NormalStrategy` is justified by cost and not by accuracy, which is not the reason anyone would have expected.

| ☑ | **R-045** | **The crate can say whether two places are connected and not how big a thing fits between them.** R-022a's `Air` answers *is this sealed*; a game asks *can the player get through*, which is a bottleneck value rather than a boolean — for a pair of chunk faces, the maximum over air paths of the minimum distance-to-solid along the path. A monotone union-find over air voxels in **descending `(field value, grid index)`** computes it; that is a total order, so no PRNG, no atomics, no `HashMap`, the same discipline the weld already uses. The output is a **6×6 symmetric aperture matrix plus a reachability mask** — the composable boundary summary, so neighbouring chunks combine with no global solve. **H:** P-49 — a drilled channel of radius 2, 4 and 8 cells read back to within one cell with every other face pair unreachable; the capped gyroid reporting all six faces mutually reachable with positive aperture on all 15 pairs; under 2× the cost of extracting the same grid. **Falsified by:** an aperture off by more than one cell where the answer is known exactly, any falsely-reachable pair (the unsound direction, and fatal for something a game would gate movement on), a gyroid pair reported unreachable (bicontinuity is a property of the surface, so that is an instrument bug), or cost at or above 2×. **Overlap named:** the connectivity half is banked; the aperture *value* and the composable 6×6 are the delta. **FINDINGS:** `M-`. | M | — |
> **DONE 2026-08-23 — M-346 / P-49 HELD on all three clauses.** C1 allowed one cell of slack and the error is **exactly zero** at r = 2, 4 and 8 cells, with 1 of 1 reachable pairs and the other fourteen unreachable on every slab; `false_reachable_pairs` and `missed_reachable_pairs` are **0 on every row**, so there is no error in either direction to characterise. C2 held on the registered fixture and the harness showed the fixture was measuring the wrong thing **before** the run: `capped_gyroid` is `max(gyroid, sphere(6))` over `[−7,7]³`, so everything outside radius 6 is air and the twelve adjacent-face pairs all read exactly **27.9971** cells = `(7√3 − 6)/h` — the shared corner sample `(7,7,7)`. An **uncapped** row was added on the identical grid, where the only route is the bicontinuous network the clause's reasoning invokes: 15 of 15 again, apertures spread over 6.39–6.84 instead of collapsing to two values. Not an amendment; the registered clause stands and the extra row is what makes it mean something. C3 worst **1.108** against a 2× bar on the expensive reading (sampling included, like for like against MC), 1.486 with the early exit disabled, 0.023–0.391× marginal. The early exit is sound rather than heuristic — each aperture is fixed at the first and therefore highest value that connected its pair — and fires after 5% of air samples on the gyroid. Air census checked against closed form exactly (585 / 2,925 / 12,545); determinism verified by two identical solves rather than inferred from the absence of a PRNG.

| ☑ | **R-044** | **Close the gap `isotopy.rs` names in its own header.** That module says it verbatim: *"The general form needs interval arithmetic over an arbitrary `F`, which this crate has no way to do — an `Sdf` hands back point values"*, so it certifies the **trilinear interpolant** and states plainly that it does not certify the analytic field against it. C2: 1.000/1.000/1.000/0.998 of sampled-free cells on the exact-distance fields — the registered reading — and 0.906–0.966 of *all* cells, the same verdict with a 0.58-point margin on `box_exact`. That is the gap its own header describes, measured. Enclosures are within ~11% of the `√3·h` optimum on the exact fields; `fbm_terrain` at 8.44h is loose for a structural reason (four octaves enclosed independently, no cancellation tracked). Nothing came out undecidable, including the noise fields: perlin's eight-corner sum is enclosed **term by term** rather than through the convex-combination bound, which is what makes it sharp. Liability recorded rather than trusted: the noise constants are transcribed from private items and guarded by a 137,842-point bit-exact comparison, max |Δ| = 0.

| ☑ | **R-039a** | **Gate the bitmap on a count, not a stopwatch.** M-348 falsified P-40's C2 — three quiet-machine runs read 1.022×, 1.184×, 1.177× against a registered 1.25×, and the recorded 1.336× was the outlier. ✗24 already earned the rule and cost a release doing it. **The count exists and is already in the CSV**: the prepass does not make a gather faster, it removes gathers, and how many run is `cells` under the scalar path and `active_cells` under the bitmap path — integers, identical on every machine. **H:** P-50 — the two gather counts as *equalities*, the bitmap's own cost as `sample_count` comparisons plus `cells_x.div_ceil(64)·cells_y·cells_z` word groups, and the same *ordered* active list element for element. **Falsified by:** any inequality.
